| DEC-63 | **Class kits: each class has a default ability, a weapon proficiency and a passive; "majority of these will not be in the MVP, but should be documented regardless"** (answers `INBOX.md` HC-Q3, and HC-Q1 in part). The owner's words: "Arcane Recall is the temporary name for the Magus revive default skill. Default skills/abilities are an additional special feature each class has. Warrior == Inspire Allies (surrounding ally units get small boost to movement speed, damage done, and reduced effect of fear debuff). Sentinel Mountain Fall (TBD name change) == Inflict fear on nearby enemies + short burst to movement speed and acceleration. Magus == Aetheric Recall. Each class has a proficiency for one weapon class. Legionnaire Velite = Light weapon Proficiency (e.g., gauntlets (such as raptor claws), light bows, daggers, and short swords), Medium Legionnaire (cannot recall assigned name) == Medium Melee Proficiency (e.g., dual blades, short axe, spear, medium swords, Regular bows), Praetorians == Heavy Weapon Proficiency (e.g., Broad/Great Sword, Battle Axe, Heavy Bows, Mace, etc.). For Sentinels, Juggernaut == Breaker Proficiency (same as Praetorian, minus the bows, addition of Great Maces, Great Spears, Great Swords, Great Axes, etc). For Colossus, Strong-Arm (name pending) Proficiency (e.g., Bastions, Default Melee (fists), TBD on others). Magus == staff proficiency across all Legends. Majority of these will not be in the MVP, but should be documented regardless. Arcane Intellect is a passive buff. Each class has a passive buff. Arcane Intellect increases strength of abilities and reduces Aether cost for each ability as they level up. Legionnaires have Warriors Calling, which increases their damage output, movement speed, and health regeneration as they level up. Sentinels have Enduring \_\_\_\_ (TBD Full Name), which increases armour strength when near death (5 or 10% of health remaining) and increases health regeneration out of combat. They are immune to fear and take less poison damage." So, with the new names (DEC-58): default abilities: Vanguard, Inspire Allies (nearby allied units get a small boost to movement speed and damage done, and a weaker fear debuff); Sentinel, Mountain Fall (name to change: fear on nearby enemies and a short burst of movement speed and acceleration); Magus, Aetheric Recall (the Magus revive). Weapon proficiencies: Zealot, Light Weapon (gauntlets such as the Raptor Talons, light bows, daggers, short swords); Templar, Medium Melee (dual blades, short axe, spear, medium swords, regular bows); Paladin, Heavy Weapon (broad and great swords, battle axe, heavy bows, mace); Bulwark, Breaker (the Paladin's list without the bows, plus great maces, great spears, great swords, great axes); Colossus, Strong-Arm (name pending: the Bastions, default melee with fists, others to be decided); every Magus Legend, Staff. Passives: Magus, Arcane Intellect (abilities grow stronger and cost less Aether as they level up); Vanguard, Warriors Calling (damage output, movement speed and health regeneration rise with level); Sentinel, Enduring (full name to be decided: more armour near death, at 5 or 10 % of health, more health regeneration out of combat, immune to fear, less poison damage). The recorder's notes, not the owner's words: "Arcane Recall" in his first sentence is read as Aetheric Recall, the Figma's name, which he also writes ("Magus == Aetheric Recall"); "raptor claws" as the Raptor Talons (`raptor-talons` on `feat/weapons`); "Medium Legionnaire (cannot recall assigned name)" as the Centurion, now Templar; "Legionnaire Velite" as the Zealot, "Praetorians" as the Paladin, "Juggernaut" as the Bulwark, "Warrior" and "Legionnaires" as the Vanguard; he says "each class has a proficiency" but lists them per Legend; no numbers are given (boosts, durations, the 5 or 10 % threshold), and which parts are MVP is not stated beyond "majority" not. The Magus passive may be the game doc's Studious (OQ-08), and these passives and default abilities differ from FR-052 and FR-053's list (Wrecking Run, Studious, Necromancy; Warrior none), which changes only through an ADR when they are built (NFR-010); he details the abilities and weapons next sprint (DEC-70). | content for abilities, weapons and passives (none built; `feat/weapons` ships `raptor-talons`, `wuuthrad` and `magus-staff`); FR-052, FR-053 (by ADR when built); references `01-classes-and-subclasses.md`, `06-abilities-and-progression.md`, `07-weapons-and-artifacts.md`; OQ-08, OQ-68; the class step's detail names in `packages/core/src/content/legends/magus.ts`; `INBOX.md` HC-Q1, HC-Q3, HC-Q5                                                      |

| DEC-65 | **The Legend step's detail rows become Ultimate / Abilities, and View Details opens a full-screen modal** (answers `INBOX.md` HC-Q5). The owner's words: "Not sure what those numbers refer to in terms of their actual respective wording, but class and subclass have different descriptions, as each legend has its own description and their own abilities. Aetheric Recall is not included as an ability in the subclass select, and this is where the ultimate would go. We should actually add a fourth row to Signature / Specialty / Ultimate, and change the titles. It should be Ultimate / Abilities, where the ultimate is at the top, and abilities 1, 2, and 3 are in the rows below. We could adjust sizing to be able to fit all 4 rows, leaving the ultimate as a larger row. View Details on the class selection opens a modal that takes up the whole page (blurs the rest of the screen and disables all keys except Escape), which shows all the details of the Default Ability, the Proficiency, and the Passive for each class. The icon on the left, and the description on the right. For the subclass, it shows each ability. Class example seen now in the Figma as View Details Example. Many items can be reused, other wording or titles can be found in these answers. No need for pixel perfect, same colours, font sizes, and a relatively close background blur should suffice. The modal can fade in quickly and fade out quickly, getting larger to fill the screen once loaded, and getting smaller to take up 80% of the screen when closed (obviously it becomes invisible and disabled when closed)." So: a class and each of its Legends have their own description and abilities; on the Legend step the detail rows are retitled Ultimate / Abilities: the ultimate on top as a larger row, then abilities 1, 2 and 3, four rows in place of Signature / Specialty / Ultimate, and Aetheric Recall is not among them; View Details on the class step opens a modal over the whole page (the rest blurred, every key but Escape disabled) with the class's Default Ability, Proficiency and Passive (DEC-63), each with its icon on the left and its description on the right, and for a Legend each of its abilities; his Figma frame "View Details Example" shows the class case; same colours and font sizes and a close background blur suffice, not pixel-perfect; it fades in quickly while growing to fill the screen, and on closing shrinks to 80 % of the screen while fading out, then is invisible and disabled. The recorder's notes: which Magus description is canonical (the class step's "With a mastery of Aether..." or the alternate layout's "The masters of Aether...") is still open, now asked by their wording (`INBOX.md` HC-Q5); whether the alternate layout's own labels change too is not stated; "quickly" gives no duration, so the motion tokens and the reduced-motion rules apply. **Appended 2026-10-29**: key V opens View Details as a full screen built from the existing components, with a vignette backdrop, and Oracle's Unison Ward is ability 1 as a preview (DEC-103).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | the Legend step's detail rows and the class step's View Details (`ui/screens/`), the spec fields they read (`packages/core/src/content/legends/`); ## Batch 2026-10-16 (PR #33 review: the minion system design, sholtzman)

| ID     | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Docs changed                                                                           |
| ------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| DEC-43 | **Hordes march in formation** (rejects the loose-flock proposal MS-5). Formation is the more sensible design and lore-accurate: a single swing of a large weapon should not wipe out a loose horde, and well-formed legions make a harder, structured fight. Formation is not rigid once combat starts: members may break it to fight, and it never stops a unit steering around an obstacle. Shape and spacing are `TUNE` content. FR-037 stands. | `architecture/18-minion-system.md` §1 (MS-23), §4, §5; `architecture/10-physics.md` §9 |
| DEC-44 | **A horde advances up its lane from where it spawns**, toward the enemy; it turns aside only to attack an enemy that is right there. A horde spawned at a tower never walks back toward its own gate.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `architecture/18-minion-system.md` §1 (MS-24), §3.2                                    |
| DEC-45 | **Every lane minion spawns as part of a horde**: mobs and their horde membership are created together, so no mob exists without its horde record.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `architecture/18-minion-system.md` §1 (MS-25), §3.2                                    |
| DEC-46 | **Developer-menu mob spawns are hordes too**: `dev-spawn-mobs` spawns its mobs as one horde, with the lane taken from the origin.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `architecture/18-minion-system.md` §3.5; `architecture/13-game-interface.md` §4        |
| DEC-47 | **Do not drop Phase 3 scope**: the forest camps, checkpoint assist spawns (sprint 3.4) and A\* for Legends (sprint 3.2) are key to gameplay and stay in Phase 3; the minion plan supersedes only the items it covers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `plan/minion-system-plan.md`; `plan/phase-3-minions-hordes-and-ai.md`                  |

Numbered DEC-43 to DEC-47 because DEC-38 to DEC-42 were already taken (skins, the Phase 1
waiver, PR #36's status rulings). Still open from the same review: the worst-lane terms and weights (MS-9) and where horde size and
cadence live (doc 18 §10). The reviewer's naming note (minions provisionally **Host**, hordes a
**Cohort** or **legion**) is not a rename yet.

## Batch 2026-10-16 (PR #35 status-effect rulings)

The owner's answers, given in chat on 2026-10-16, to the questions raised by PR #35
(`feat/status-effects`, SCRUM-228). They cover the PR's own `INBOX.md` item, questions (1)–(3)
as they stood at `3993163`, and the review's owner calls (items 17 and 18), which became question
(4) of that item at `09a48a6`. The item's question (5), the list of what is not built yet, was not
put to the owner and stays open. The owner's wording was ambiguous on three points, so he was asked
follow-up questions; each answer was a choice among options, quoted below as "the owner's picks".
DEC-38 (Legend skins, `docs/sprint-2-requirements-and-planning`) and DEC-39 (the Phase 1 waiver,
reserved in PR #16) are taken elsewhere.

| ID     | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Affects                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| DEC-40 | **Status modifiers multiply, both per stack and across statuses, as architecture 05 §8 sketches (`op: 'mul'`).** The owner's words: "yes, multiply." Two `enraged` stacks give ×1.15² damage. Concussion with a 40% slow gives ×0.7 × 0.6 = ×0.42 move speed, not the additive ×0.30 (review item 1, which asked the question). This answers question (1) of PR #35's INBOX item. The PR adopted it in `09a48a6`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | architecture 05 §8 (unchanged: confirmed); PR #35 `components/StatusEffects.ts`; `suggestions/more-abilities.md` §7 and `suggestions/balancing.md` (the "per stack" values)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| DEC-41 | **Two of PR #35's departures from the design become the design (as reviewed at `3993163`).** (a) `combat/StatusEffectApplier` is the one way a status lands. It resolves the status by id; refuses missing targets, dead targets and targets without `StatusEffects` (structures); and emits `status-applied`. So the applier, not `StatusEffectSystem`, emits that event. (b) `StatusEffects` recomputes its cached multipliers and flags whenever its list changes, instead of `StatusEffectSystem` recomputing them every tick. The owner's words: "yes, approve" for each. Not part of the ruling: review item 18's implementation notes, kept on the PR. Open: whether the approval also covers `09a48a6`'s wider applier, which also removes and expires statuses (emitting `status-expired`) and is built by `DefaultSystemPipeline`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | architecture 05 §3 (effects call `StatusEffects.apply`), §8, 03 §4 events table (`status-applied` / `status-expired` emitter), 17 R9 (owner); `suggestions/more-abilities.md` §8; PR #35                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| DEC-42 | **Stun and blind.** The owner's words: "2 seconds. blind makes aim slightly wobbly (like stun), but also causes the screen to have a heavy white vignette that dissipates over 2-3 seconds. yes, aimed projectiles miss too". The owner's picks the same day: "Stun default (Recommended)" for the 2 seconds; "Wobble causes misses"; "Yes, stun wobbles too". This rules three things. (a) **Stun** lasts 2 s by default, in place of more-abilities §7's "0.5–1 s per source", and a stunned unit's aim wobbles too. (b) **Blind** makes aim wobble, and the wobble is what makes basic attacks and aimed projectiles miss. They veer off and usually miss, but a lucky shot can still land, so a miss is not guaranteed. Blind's 1.5 s stays. (c) A blinded human player's screen gets a heavy white vignette that fades over 2–3 s. The recorder's notes, not the owner's: the 2 s carries `TUNE` like every content number; under this log's precedence, stun keeps "cannot move, attack, cast or interact" until the first open question is answered. Open: whether a stunned unit can still act; whether the per-source stuns stand, with 2 s only the default (`balancing.md`: Blade Runner 0.5 s, Meteor Fall 1 s, Nova 0.5 s; more-abilities §2: Riposte 0.75 s); how large the wobble is ("slightly"); whether the vignette replaces blind's −70% vision radius (more-abilities §7) or adds to it; and whether the vignette's 2–3 s fade starts when blind lands or when it ends. Architecture: how the wobble applies to melee basic attacks, which are reach-based today (it must come from the seeded RNG). | `suggestions/more-abilities.md` §7 `stun` and `blind` rows, §2 (Riposte); `suggestions/balancing.md` (per-source stuns); PR #35 `content/status-effects/stun.ts`, `blind.ts`, and `systems/CombatSystem.ts` with the 03 §4 `attack-missed` row (its guaranteed miss as of `09a48a6`); architecture 05 §2–§3 (`aim`), §8, §10; 08 §4, §8 (canvas overlay) or 13 (HUD) for the vignette (owner); `apps/client/src/game/views/` |

| DEC-14 | **All four map-generator refinements signed off** (2026-10-06), so they are now the design: (a) `campsPerSide` is split as evenly as possible over the corridors, leftovers to the middle ones, never rejected for not dividing; (b) presets give a lane profile, a symmetric rift width profile and west out-of-bounds polygons in fractions of the world, never per-lane paths; (c) exits are spread evenly over an arc, one gate centred on each, walls fill the rest, a minimum gate opening rejects configs whose turrets would seal a gate, lanes leave each gate straight for an apron; (d) lanes are traced for Light and mirrored, and `WorldMap` reference-counts footprint stamps over a separate terrain layer. | `docs/architecture/06-map-and-minimap.md` (folded into §1–§8), ADR 0009 status, `docs/reviews/2026-10-05-map-code-audit.md` §4/§6 |

### New ideas logged (not yet scheduled)

- **Tower Guardians** — 4 specialised heavy-class guards per tower (and one on each side of every
  Stronghold gate); higher damage/health than minions; ranged and melee attacks; fight within a
  10 m radius and never chase; fallen guards are replaced after 5 minutes (as many as fell).
  This refines the game doc's "guards respawn every 2 minutes" and goes to `02-entities`,
  `07-structures` §4 and `GuardController` when scheduled (**MVP-if-time**; the base guard
  behaviour is MVP).
- **Weapon upgrades and scaling** — profile-level unlocks configured in loadouts; in-match XP
  unlocks tiers 1–5 up to a "Prime" form with visual changes. **Post-MVP** (backlog).
- **Visual feedback at level checkpoints** (every 10 levels; peak at 50) and purchasable skins.
  **Post-MVP**.
- **Team-specific skins** — Light and Dark Legends are mechanically identical (hitboxes,
  weapons, abilities); only appearance differs per team. Already the art-theme rule; recorded as
  an invariant for content: team never changes a definition's gameplay fields.
- **Interface flow** (home → loading → character select → pre-launch → match loading → match)
  recorded in [14-interface-and-screen-flow.md](14-interface-and-screen-flow.md).
